<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We disprove the stable Morse lower bound for nondegenerate Hamiltonian fixed points by constructing a simply connected closed Kähler manifold with sixteen fewer fixed points than its stable Morse number. All the corresponding periodic orbits are contractible. The construction combines a Hamiltonian involution with integral homology torsion at two different primes; its proof uses finite-dimensional Morse theory and complex blowups.

- **claim-stable-morse-counterexample** (counterexample, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There exist a simply connected closed Kähler manifold $(M,\omega)$ and a smooth one-periodic Hamiltonian $H$ such that every fixed point of $\phi_H^1$ is nondegenerate and $$\#\mathop{\mathrm{Fix}}_0(\phi_H^1;H)=\#\mathop{\mathrm{Fix}}(\phi_H^1)=\mathop{\mathrm{SM}}(M)-16.$$ One may take $\dim_{\mathbb R}M=1412$, with $\mathop{\mathrm{SM}}(M)=318968$ and $\#\mathop{\mathrm{Fix}}(\phi_H^1)=318952$.
  - Explanation: A simply connected closed Kähler manifold admits a Hamiltonian with nondegenerate, contractible fixed-point loops whose count is strictly below the geometric stable Morse number.
- **claim-equivalent-infinity-conventions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-infinity-conventions)): Fix a Riemannian metric on a closed smooth manifold $W$ and the Euclidean metric on $\mathbb R^k$, where $k>0$. Suppose $Q$ is a nondegenerate quadratic form on $\mathbb R^k$ and $F\colon W\times\mathbb R^k\to\mathbb R$ is a smooth function with nondegenerate critical points such that $$\sup_{W\times\mathbb R^k}\\|dF-dQ\\|<\infty$$ in the product metric. Then there is a smooth function $\widetilde F$ equal to $F$ near all its critical points, with no additional critical points, and equal to $Q$ outside a compact set. Consequently allowing these bounded differential perturbations in the definition of the stable Morse number does not change its value.
  - Explanation: A bounded differential perturbation of a nondegenerate quadratic form can be cut off without changing any critical point or adding new ones. The geometric stable Morse minimum is therefore unchanged.
- **claim-integral-stable-morse-lower-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-stable-lower)): For every connected closed smooth manifold $W$, $$\mathop{\mathrm{SM}}(W)\geq \lambda(W).$$
  - Explanation: Integral homology rank and degreewise torsion generator counts give a lower bound for every admissible stabilized Morse function.
- **claim-smale-morse-attainment** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-smale)): Let $W$ be a simply connected closed smooth manifold of dimension $D\geq9$. There is an ordinary Morse function on $W$ with $$r_i(W)+t_i(W)+t_{i-1}(W)$$ critical points of index $i$ for every $i$. In particular, $$\label{eq:simply-connected-morse} \mathop{\mathrm{SM}}(W)=\mathop{\mathrm{Morse}}(W)=\lambda(W).$$
  - Explanation: Under simple connectivity and the stated dimension hypothesis, an ordinary Morse function attains the integral homological lower bound, with the prescribed critical-point count in each index.
- **claim-bielliptic-surface-homology** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-torsion-surfaces)): The surfaces $S_2$ and $S_3$ admit holomorphic embeddings into $\mathbb{CP}^{44}$ and $\mathbb{CP}^{164}$, respectively. Set $$T_2=(\mathbb Z/2)^2,\qquad T_3=\mathbb Z/3.$$ Their integral homology is $$\label{eq:surface-homology} H_i(S_p;\mathbb Z)\cong \begin{cases} \mathbb Z,&i=0,4,\\ \mathbb Z^2\oplus T_p,&i=1,2,\\ \mathbb Z^2,&i=3,\\ 0,&\text{otherwise}. \end{cases}$$ In particular, $b(S_p)=8$.
  - Explanation: The quotient surfaces have explicit holomorphic projective embeddings and integral homology carrying the required torsion at different primes.
- **claim-integral-blowup-decomposition** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-blowup)): Let $A$ be a compact connected Kähler manifold, and let $Z\subset A$ be a closed connected complex submanifold of complex codimension $c\geq2$. Its complex blow-up $\pi:\widehat A\to A$ is compact, connected, and Kähler. If $A$ is simply connected, then so is $\widehat A$. For every integer $i$ there is an isomorphism of abelian groups $$\label{eq:blowup-homology} H_i(\widehat A;\mathbb Z) \cong H_i(A;\mathbb Z)\oplus \bigoplus_{j=1}^{c-1}H_{i-2j}(Z;\mathbb Z).$$
  - Explanation: Complex blow-up preserves the required geometric properties and adds shifted copies of the center's integral homology, retaining its torsion as direct summands.
- **claim-mixed-prime-kahler-manifold** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-torsion-space)): For the fixed integer $N\geq704$, there is a simply connected compact Kähler manifold $X$ of complex dimension $N$ obtained by blowing up $\mathbb{CP}^N$ along disjoint copies of $$Z_2=S_2,\qquad Z_3=S_3\times\mathbb{CP}^1\times\mathbb{CP}^1.$$ Set $L=N-3$. Its only nonzero integral homology torsion groups occur in degrees $1+2j$ and $2+2j$, where $1\leq j\leq L$, and are $$\label{eq:X-torsion} \mathop{\mathrm{Tor}}H_{1+2j}(X;\mathbb Z)\cong\mathop{\mathrm{Tor}}H_{2+2j}(X;\mathbb Z) \cong(\mathbb Z/2)^{a_j}\oplus(\mathbb Z/3)^{q_j},$$ where $$\label{eq:torsion-profiles} a_j=2,\qquad q_j= \begin{cases} 1,&j=1,L,\\ 3,&j=2,L-1,\\ 4,&3\leq j\leq L-2. \end{cases}$$ Moreover, $$\label{eq:rank-X} b(X)=41N-183.$$
  - Explanation: Blowing up projective space along disjoint centers produces a simply connected Kähler manifold with explicitly controlled torsion profiles and total homology rank.
- **claim-no-short-nonconstant-periods** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-short-period)): Let $V$ be a smooth vector field on a closed smooth manifold $P$, and let $\psi^s$ denote its flow. There is a number $\tau>0$ such that, for every $0<T<\tau$, $$\psi^T(x)=x \quad\Longrightarrow\quad V(x)=0.$$
  - Explanation: A smooth vector field on a closed manifold has a positive period threshold below which every returning trajectory is an equilibrium.
- **claim-involution-fixed-point-realization** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-involution)): Let $(P,\omega)$ be a closed symplectic manifold, and let $g:P\to P$ be a Hamiltonian diffeomorphism satisfying $g^2=\mathrm{id}_P$. Write $$\mathop{\mathrm{Fix}}(g)=F_1\sqcup\cdots\sqcup F_r$$ for its connected fixed components. For each $j$, choose a smooth Morse function $h_j:F_j\to\mathbb R$. There exists a $g$-invariant smooth function $f:P\to\mathbb R$ with $f\|_{F_j}=h_j$ such that, for all sufficiently small $\epsilon>0$, the map $$\Phi_\epsilon=\psi^\epsilon\circ g,$$ where $\psi^s$ is the Hamiltonian flow of $f$, satisfies $$\label{eq:involution-fixed-set} \mathop{\mathrm{Fix}}(\Phi_\epsilon) =\bigsqcup_{j=1}^r\operatorname{Crit}(h_j).$$ Every fixed point of $\Phi_\epsilon$ is nondegenerate. Moreover, $\Phi_\epsilon$ is the time-one map of a smooth one-periodic Hamiltonian. If $P$ is simply connected, all its fixed-point loops for this Hamiltonian are contractible.
  - Explanation: An invariant Hamiltonian extension of Morse functions on the fixed components yields a small perturbation whose fixed points are precisely the chosen critical points. They are nondegenerate, and simple connectivity ensures contractibility of the specified fixed-point loops.
- **claim-exceptional-curve-involution** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-surface)): Let $Y$ be the complex blow-up of $\mathbb{CP}^1\times\mathbb{CP}^1$ at the four points $$\{0,\infty\}\times\{0,\infty\}.$$ There is a circle-invariant Kähler form $\omega_Y$ for which the lifted diagonal circle action is Hamiltonian. Its half-turn $g_Y$ has precisely four fixed components, the exceptional curves $E_1,\ldots,E_4$, each isomorphic to $\mathbb{CP}^1$. Along each $E_j$, the derivative of $g_Y$ is the identity on the tangent bundle and minus the identity on the symplectic normal bundle. The surface $Y$ is simply connected, and $$\label{eq:Y-homology} H_i(Y;\mathbb Z)\cong \begin{cases} \mathbb Z,&i=0,4,\\ \mathbb Z^6,&i=2,\\ 0,&\text{otherwise}. \end{cases}$$
  - Explanation: The lifted half-turn on the blown-up surface fixes exactly the exceptional curves. Its tangent and normal actions have the signs needed for the perturbation argument, and the surface has free integral homology.
- **claim-product-fixed-point-realization** (construction, [source](#stmt-cor-product-realization)): Let $(X,\omega_X)$ be a connected closed simply connected Kähler manifold, let $(Y,\omega_Y)$ be the surface in Proposition <a href="#prop:surface" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:surface">12</a>, and give $M=X\times Y$ the product symplectic form $\omega_X\oplus\omega_Y$. Put $C=X\times\mathbb{CP}^1$. For every smooth Morse function $h:C\to\mathbb R$, there is a smooth one-periodic Hamiltonian $H$ on $M$ such that every fixed point of its time-one map is nondegenerate and contractible for its Hamiltonian path, and $$\#\mathop{\mathrm{Fix}}(\phi_H^1)=\#\mathop{\mathrm{Fix}}_0(\phi_H^1;H) =4\,\#\operatorname{Crit}(h).$$
  - Explanation: Every chosen Morse function on the component product gives a Hamiltonian on the ambient product whose fixed-point count is the combined critical-point count of the fixed components.
- **claim-exact-morse-count-gap** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-gap)): For the manifolds in <a href="#eq:counts-products" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:counts-products">[eq:counts-products]</a>, $$\label{eq:counts-values} \lambda(C)=114N-518,\qquad \lambda(M)=456N-2056.$$ In particular, $$\label{eq:counts-gap} \lambda(M)-4\lambda(C)=16.$$
  - Explanation: The product homology calculation yields the stated affine Morse invariants and a positive gap between the ambient invariant and the combined component invariants.

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-blowup",
        "exact_quote": "Let $A$ be a compact connected Kähler manifold, and let $Z\\subset A$ be a closed connected complex submanifold of complex codimension $c\\geq2$. Its complex blow-up $\\pi:\\widehat A\\to A$ is compact, connected, and Kähler. If $A$ is simply connected, then so is $\\widehat A$. For every integer $i$ there is an isomorphism of abelian groups $$\\label{eq:blowup-homology}\n H_i(\\widehat A;\\mathbb Z)\n \\cong H_i(A;\\mathbb Z)\\oplus\n \\bigoplus_{j=1}^{c-1}H_{i-2j}(Z;\\mathbb Z).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-347.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-blowup",
          "exact_quote": "Let $A$ be a compact connected Kähler manifold, and let $Z\\subset A$ be a closed connected complex submanifold of complex codimension $c\\geq2$. Its complex blow-up $\\pi:\\widehat A\\to A$ is compact, connected, and Kähler. If $A$ is simply connected, then so is $\\widehat A$. For every integer $i$ there is an isomorphism of abelian groups $$\\label{eq:blowup-homology}\n H_i(\\widehat A;\\mathbb Z)\n \\cong H_i(A;\\mathbb Z)\\oplus\n \\bigoplus_{j=1}^{c-1}H_{i-2j}(Z;\\mathbb Z).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-347.3"
        }
      ],

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-torsion-space",
        "exact_quote": "For the fixed integer $N\\geq704$, there is a simply connected compact Kähler manifold $X$ of complex dimension $N$ obtained by blowing up $\\mathbb{CP}^N$ along disjoint copies of $$Z_2=S_2,\\qquad Z_3=S_3\\times\\mathbb{CP}^1\\times\\mathbb{CP}^1.$$ Set $L=N-3$. Its only nonzero integral homology torsion groups occur in degrees $1+2j$ and $2+2j$, where $1\\leq j\\leq L$, and are $$\\label{eq:X-torsion}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Tor}}H_{1+2j}(X;\\mathbb Z)\\cong\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tor}}H_{2+2j}(X;\\mathbb Z)\n \\cong(\\mathbb Z/2)^{a_j}\\oplus(\\mathbb Z/3)^{q_j},$$ where $$\\label{eq:torsion-profiles}\n a_j=2,\\qquad\n q_j=\n \\begin{cases}\n  1,&j=1,L,\\\\\n  3,&j=2,L-1,\\\\\n  4,&3\\leq j\\leq L-2.\n \\end{cases}$$ Moreover, $$\\label{eq:rank-X}\n b(X)=41N-183.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-347.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-torsion-space",
          "exact_quote": "For the fixed integer $N\\geq704$, there is a simply connected compact Kähler manifold $X$ of complex dimension $N$ obtained by blowing up $\\mathbb{CP}^N$ along disjoint copies of $$Z_2=S_2,\\qquad Z_3=S_3\\times\\mathbb{CP}^1\\times\\mathbb{CP}^1.$$ Set $L=N-3$. Its only nonzero integral homology torsion groups occur in degrees $1+2j$ and $2+2j$, where $1\\leq j\\leq L$, and are $$\\label{eq:X-torsion}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Tor}}H_{1+2j}(X;\\mathbb Z)\\cong\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tor}}H_{2+2j}(X;\\mathbb Z)\n \\cong(\\mathbb Z/2)^{a_j}\\oplus(\\mathbb Z/3)^{q_j},$$ where $$\\label{eq:torsion-profiles}\n a_j=2,\\qquad\n q_j=\n \\begin{cases}\n  1,&j=1,L,\\\\\n  3,&j=2,L-1,\\\\\n  4,&3\\leq j\\leq L-2.\n \\end{cases}$$ Moreover, $$\\label{eq:rank-X}\n b(X)=41N-183.$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-involution",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(P,\\omega)$ be a closed symplectic manifold, and let $g:P\\to P$ be a Hamiltonian diffeomorphism satisfying $g^2=\\mathrm{id}_P$. Write $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{Fix}}(g)=F_1\\sqcup\\cdots\\sqcup F_r$$ for its connected fixed components. For each $j$, choose a smooth Morse function $h_j:F_j\\to\\mathbb R$. There exists a $g$-invariant smooth function $f:P\\to\\mathbb R$ with $f|_{F_j}=h_j$ such that, for all sufficiently small $\\epsilon>0$, the map $$\\Phi_\\epsilon=\\psi^\\epsilon\\circ g,$$ where $\\psi^s$ is the Hamiltonian flow of $f$, satisfies $$\\label{eq:involution-fixed-set}\n  \\mathop{\\mathrm{Fix}}(\\Phi_\\epsilon)\n    =\\bigsqcup_{j=1}^r\\operatorname{Crit}(h_j).$$ Every fixed point of $\\Phi_\\epsilon$ is nondegenerate. Moreover, $\\Phi_\\epsilon$ is the time-one map of a smooth one-periodic Hamiltonian. If $P$ is simply connected, all its fixed-point loops for this Hamiltonian are contractible.",
        "source_id": "PM-347.3"
      },
      "evidence": [

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-surface",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Y$ be the complex blow-up of $\\mathbb{CP}^1\\times\\mathbb{CP}^1$ at the four points $$\\{0,\\infty\\}\\times\\{0,\\infty\\}.$$ There is a circle-invariant Kähler form $\\omega_Y$ for which the lifted diagonal circle action is Hamiltonian. Its half-turn $g_Y$ has precisely four fixed components, the exceptional curves $E_1,\\ldots,E_4$, each isomorphic to $\\mathbb{CP}^1$. Along each $E_j$, the derivative of $g_Y$ is the identity on the tangent bundle and minus the identity on the symplectic normal bundle. The surface $Y$ is simply connected, and $$\\label{eq:Y-homology}\n  H_i(Y;\\mathbb Z)\\cong\n  \\begin{cases}\n    \\mathbb Z,&i=0,4,\\\\\n    \\mathbb Z^6,&i=2,\\\\\n    0,&\\text{otherwise}.\n  \\end{cases}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-347.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-product-realization",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(X,\\omega_X)$ be a connected closed simply connected Kähler manifold, let $(Y,\\omega_Y)$ be the surface in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:surface\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:surface\">12</a>, and give $M=X\\times Y$ the product symplectic form $\\omega_X\\oplus\\omega_Y$. Put $C=X\\times\\mathbb{CP}^1$. For every smooth Morse function $h:C\\to\\mathbb R$, there is a smooth one-periodic Hamiltonian $H$ on $M$ such that every fixed point of its time-one map is nondegenerate and contractible for its Hamiltonian path, and $$\\#\\mathop{\\mathrm{Fix}}(\\phi_H^1)=\\#\\mathop{\\mathrm{Fix}}_0(\\phi_H^1;H)\n       =4\\,\\#\\operatorname{Crit}(h).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-347.3"
      },

Put $C=X\\times\\mathbb{CP}^1$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Geometric stable Morse number** ([source](#stmt-auto-definition-1)): Let $W$ be a connected closed smooth manifold. Its *stable Morse number* $\mathop{\mathrm{SM}}(W)$ is the minimum of $\#\mathop{\mathrm{Crit}}(F)$ over all integers $k\geq0$ and smooth Morse functions $$F:W\times\mathbb R^k\longrightarrow\mathbb R$$ such that, when $k>0$, there are a nondegenerate quadratic form $Q$ on $\mathbb R^k$ and $R>0$ with $F(x,z)=Q(z)$ for every $x\in W$ and $\|z\|\geq R$. The signature of $Q$ is unrestricted. For $k=0$, there is no condition at infinity. The ordinary Morse number $\mathop{\mathrm{Morse}}(W)$ is the same minimum restricted to $k=0$.
- **Ordinary Morse number** ([source](#stmt-auto-definition-1)): The ordinary Morse number $\mathop{\mathrm{Morse}}(W)$ is the same minimum restricted to $k=0$.
- **Nondegenerate Hamiltonian fixed point** ([source](#eq:proposed-bound)): A fixed point $x$ of $\phi_H^1$ is *nondegenerate* if $1$ is not an eigenvalue of $d_x\phi_H^1$.
- **Contractible Hamiltonian fixed-point set** ([source](#eq:proposed-bound)): Write $$\mathop{\mathrm{Fix}}_0(\phi_H^1;H)=\{x\in\mathop{\mathrm{Fix}}(\phi_H^1):t\mapsto\phi_H^t(x)\text{ is contractible in }M\}.$$
- **Total homology rank and torsion generator numbers** ([source](#pm-h-4)): For a closed manifold $W$, let $b(W)$ be its total integral homology rank, and let $t_i(W)$ be the minimum number of generators of $\mathop{\mathrm{Tor}}H_i(W;\mathbb Z)$.
- **Integral Morse lower-bound invariant** ([source](#pm-h-4)): The quantity $$\lambda(W)=b(W)+2\sum_i t_i(W)$$ is a lower bound for $\mathop{\mathrm{SM}}(W)$.
- **Component and ambient products** ([source](#eq:counts-products)): Set $$\label{eq:counts-products} C=X\times\mathbb{CP}^1,\qquad M=X\times Y.$$
- **Minimal Chern number** ([source](#stmt-rem-periodic-bound)): The *minimal Chern number* $\nu$ is the positive generator of the subgroup $\langle c_1(TM),\pi_2(M)\rangle\subset\mathbb Z$ when that subgroup is nonzero.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-cutoff-at-infinity** [claim](#stmt-lem-infinity-conventions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-infinity-conventions): Control the perturbation's growth and cut it off on an annulus where the quadratic differential dominates. -> An exactly quadratic function with the same critical points and local behavior.
- **step-integral-morse-lower-bound** [claim](#stmt-prop-stable-lower) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-stable-lower): Truncate the quadratic ends, identify the endpoint relative homology, and count free chain ranks using torsion invariant factors. -> The integral lower bound for the geometric stable Morse number.
- **step-remove-extreme-handles** [claim](#stmt-thm-smale) / [proof](#pm-h-14): Use simple connectivity of the closed manifold to express free generators through attaching words, insert cancelling pairs, and remove handles of index one and coindex one. -> A decomposition with no handles of index one or coindex one.
- **step-find-unit-handle-coefficient** [claim](#stmt-thm-smale) / [proof](#pm-h-15): Compare boundary image ranks with torsion generator counts and realize a unit Smith factor by handle basis changes. -> A pair of adjacent-index handles with algebraic intersection number equal to a unit.
- **step-geometric-handle-cancellation** [claim](#stmt-thm-smale) / [proof](#pm-h-16): Remove opposite-sign intersections by the Whitney argument; in the low-index case use the simply connected belt-sphere complement and relative homotopy before isotopy. -> A single geometric intersection allowing handle cancellation.
- **step-smale-attainment** [claim](#stmt-thm-smale) / [proof](#pm-h-17): Cancel excess pairs until the handle count reaches the homological invariant, then read off the Morse index counts. -> An ordinary Morse function attaining the geometric stable Morse minimum.
- **step-quotient-surfaces** [claim](#stmt-lem-torsion-surfaces) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-torsion-surfaces): Embed the elliptic products, separate quotient orbits by products of linear polynomials, and compute homology using deck-group abelianization and duality. -> Projectively embedded surfaces with the stated integral torsion groups.
- **step-integral-blowup** [claim](#stmt-prop-blowup) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-blowup): Construct the Kähler form and verify simple connectivity, then split integral cohomology using the exceptional divisor and apply duality. -> Preserved geometric properties and the integral blow-up homology decomposition.
- **step-build-mixed-prime-manifold** [claim](#stmt-prop-torsion-space) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-torsion-space): Place the centers in disjoint projective subspaces, perform the blow-ups, and compute the shifted torsion profiles by the integral Künneth formula. -> The simply connected Kähler manifold with the prescribed rank and mixed-prime profiles.
- **step-short-period-threshold** [claim](#stmt-lem-short-period) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-short-period): Choose finitely many nested coordinate balls and uniform speed and Lipschitz bounds, then compare the maximum trajectory speed with its average over a return. -> A positive threshold excluding nonconstant short periodic trajectories.
- **step-perturb-involution** [claim](#stmt-prop-involution) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-involution): Average extensions of the component Morse functions, use commutation and the short-period threshold to locate fixed points, and check the tangent and normal derivative blocks. -> A smooth periodic Hamiltonian realizing exactly the component critical points as nondegenerate fixed points.
- **step-build-surface-involution** [claim](#stmt-prop-surface) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-surface): Lift the diagonal circle action through the point blow-ups, average a Kähler form, and inspect the half-turn in the local blow-up model. -> A Hamiltonian involution fixing precisely the exceptional projective lines.
- **step-product-realization** [claim](#stmt-cor-product-realization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-product-realization): Take the product involution and transport the same Morse function to each fixed component before applying the perturbation construction. -> The required combined fixed-point count with contractible loops.
- **step-compute-product-gap** [claim](#stmt-prop-gap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-gap): Convolve the prime multiplicity profiles with the free product homology, take the degreewise maximum, and compute the endpoint losses. -> The exact component and ambient invariants and their positive difference.
- **step-assemble-counterexample** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-gap-2): Choose a component Morse function attaining the homological invariant, realize its critical points dynamically, and compare with the ambient stable Morse number. -> The stated nondegenerate Hamiltonian counterexample with contractible periodic orbits.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-geometric-stable-lower-bound**: Integral homology gives a lower bound for the geometric stable Morse number, with torsion generators counted separately in each integer degree.
- **inv-smale-attainment**: The ordinary and stable Morse numbers both attain the integral homology lower bound under the stated simple connectivity and dimension hypotheses.
- **inv-involution-localization**: A sufficiently small invariant Hamiltonian perturbation of the Hamiltonian involution has precisely the prescribed component critical points as fixed points, all nondegenerate.
- **inv-mixed-prime-gap**: For the constructed products, the ambient integral Morse lower bound strictly exceeds the combined component bound.
- **inv-contractible-counterexample**: The constructed Hamiltonian has only nondegenerate fixed points, every corresponding periodic orbit is contractible, and its fixed-point count is below the geometric stable Morse number.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-algebraic-upper-bound**: Treat a small integral chain complex as a geometric stable Morse upper bound. -> The chain complex does not itself supply an admissible smooth Morse function attaining its rank.
- **anti-unrestricted-smale-attainment**: Apply the Morse attainment equality to arbitrary closed manifolds. -> The cited attainment theorem requires simple connectivity and its stated dimension range.
- **anti-arbitrary-perturbation-size**: Use the exact fixed-point localization for an arbitrary positive perturbation parameter. -> The short-period argument and the derivative checks only establish the conclusion for sufficiently small parameters.
- **anti-cyclic-grading-substitution**: Replace integer-degree torsion generator counts with parity-grouped counts when evaluating the geometric stable Morse number. -> Combining degrees permits different mixed-prime generator savings and changes the bound being computed.
- **anti-numerical-geometric-certificate**: Certify the full Hamiltonian counterexample by checking only the finite torsion-profile arithmetic. -> Arithmetic agreement does not establish smooth Morse attainment, the Hamiltonian realization, nondegeneracy, or contractibility.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The full counterexample is a geometric existence result involving smooth manifolds, Morse functions, Hamiltonian flows, and sufficiently small perturbations. The assembly explicitly requires: “Choose a Morse function on $C$ with exactly $\lambda(C)$ critical points.” The realization proposition then asserts: “Every fixed point of $\Phi_\epsilon$ is nondegenerate.” Finite evaluation of the torsion-profile formulas checks the numerical comparison but cannot certify these smooth existence and dynamical conclusions. No finite executable construction of the required smooth input and its verified perturbation threshold is supplied.

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-geometric-stabilization** (formal_scope): The stable Morse minimum concerns actual smooth Morse functions with the prescribed quadratic behavior at infinity. A small algebraic chain complex alone does not establish geometric attainment. Blocked use: ['Claiming an upper bound for the stable Morse number solely from algebraic chain ranks.', 'Replacing the admissible functions by functions with unrestricted behavior at infinity.']
- **limit-bounded-differential-cutoff** (hypothesis): The equivalence of conventions at infinity requires a uniformly bounded differential perturbation of a nondegenerate quadratic form and nondegenerate critical points. Blocked use: ['Applying the cutoff conclusion to an unbounded differential perturbation.', 'Assuming a degenerate quadratic form provides the same control of critical points at infinity.']
- **limit-morse-attainment-regime** (hypothesis): The stated geometric attainment theorem requires simple connectivity, closedness, smoothness, and the dimension hypothesis $D\geq9$. The general stable Morse comparison supplies a lower bound without asserting attainment outside this regime. Blocked use: ['Equating the ordinary Morse number, stable Morse number, and homological invariant for arbitrary closed manifolds.', 'Applying the supplied handle cancellation proof below its stated dimension range.']
- **limit-integral-blowup-center** (hypothesis): The blow-up statement assumes a compact connected Kähler ambient manifold and a closed connected complex center of complex codimension $c\geq2$. Preservation of simple connectivity additionally assumes it for the ambient manifold. Blocked use: ['Transferring the stated homology decomposition to an arbitrary surgery or a noncomplex center.', 'Inferring simple connectivity of the blow-up without the ambient hypothesis.']
- **limit-disjoint-projective-centers** (hypothesis): The mixed-prime construction uses disjoint embedded centers and the integer hypothesis $N\geq704$. These conditions support the specified successive blow-ups and torsion profiles. Blocked use: ['Using the same profiles after blowing up intersecting centers.', 'Claiming that this construction provides the same example for a smaller projective ambient space.']
- **limit-integer-graded-prime-profiles** (formal_scope): The generator savings depend on the distribution of torsion at distinct primes in each integer degree. Total torsion multiplicities without their degrees do not determine the product Morse-count gap. Blocked use: ['Adding prime-specific generator counts instead of allowing cyclic invariant factors to combine distinct primes.', 'Replacing the degreewise profiles by their aggregate totals before computing the geometric Morse invariant.']
- **limit-free-product-homology** (hypothesis): The convolution calculation uses product factors with free integral homology. Its omission of torsion-product terms is justified for these factors. Blocked use: ['Applying the same convolution formula unchanged when a replacement product factor has torsion in integral homology.', 'Treating rational homology ranks as sufficient input for the mixed-prime calculation.']
- **limit-short-period-compactness** (hypothesis): The short-period exclusion concerns a smooth vector field on a closed manifold. The threshold is supplied for that vector field through coordinate speed and Lipschitz bounds. Blocked use: ['Using the stated lemma without closedness or smoothness.', 'Assuming a common numerical period threshold for unrelated vector fields.']
- **limit-hamiltonian-involution-input** (hypothesis): The fixed-point realization starts with a Hamiltonian involution and smooth Morse functions on its connected fixed components. Blocked use: ['Applying the realization to an arbitrary diffeomorphism lacking the involution property.', 'Replacing the Hamiltonian involution by a merely symplectic map while retaining the claimed Hamiltonian realization.', 'Using degenerate component critical points while claiming the same nondegeneracy conclusion.']
- **limit-small-perturbation-and-extension** (hypothesis): The exact fixed-point description and full nondegeneracy require the invariant extension and a sufficiently small positive perturbation parameter. The period restriction and derivative restrictions must both hold. Blocked use: ['Assuming the same fixed-point count for an arbitrary perturbation size.', 'Extending component functions without invariance and then assuming commutation with the involution.', 'Checking only the derivative tangent to the fixed components.']
- **limit-half-turn-fixed-locus** (edge_case): The exceptional curves are fixed components of the half-turn. The full circle action need not fix those curves pointwise. Blocked use: ['Substituting the fixed locus of the full circle action for that of its half-turn.', 'Counting only circle-fixed points when applying the component Morse construction.']
- **limit-contractibility-of-specified-path** (hypothesis): Contractibility concerns loops along the specified Hamiltonian path. The general involution proposition guarantees it under simple connectivity, while the product proof also supplies a contraction within an exceptional projective line. Blocked use: ['Inferring contractibility for arbitrary Hamiltonian paths solely from equality of their time-one maps.', 'Dropping simple connectivity from the general proposition without supplying a separate loop contraction.']
- **limit-existential-counterexample-scope** (counterexample_scope): The main theorem supplies a constructed counterexample to the unrestricted stable Morse lower bound. It does not assert failure of that bound for every Hamiltonian or every manifold. Blocked use: ['Concluding that all stable Morse lower bounds fail.', 'Transferring the displayed deficit to Hamiltonians outside the constructed family.']
- **limit-cyclic-integral-bound** (formal_scope): The counterexample separates the integer-graded geometric Morse count from the cyclically graded integral Floer count. It does not contradict the established integral Floer bound. Blocked use: ['Presenting the construction as a violation of the cyclically graded integral lower bound.', 'Identifying parity-grouped torsion generator counts with the sum of the integer-degree generator counts.']
- **limit-finite-arithmetic-versus-geometric-realization** (nonconstructive): The finite torsion-profile arithmetic can be checked independently, but such a check does not certify the smooth manifold construction, the handle isotopies, or the Hamiltonian realization. Those stages require the geometric existence arguments. Blocked use: ['Treating agreement of finite count formulas as a certificate for the entire counterexample.', 'Claiming an explicit encoded Morse function or a numerical perturbation threshold from the count calculation alone.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We disprove the stable Morse lower bound for nondegenerate Hamiltonian fixed points by constructing a simply connected closed Kähler manifold with sixteen fewer fixed points than its stable Morse number. All the corresponding periodic orbits are contractible. The construction combines a Hamiltonian involution with integral homology torsion at two different primes; its proof uses finite-dimensional Morse theory and complex blowups.
